#5259. Coinbase Reveals November Delisting Schedule, Issues Warning on Solana Token Listing

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Coinbase crypto exchange has shared upcoming changes to its cryptocurrency listings for the month of November, including its delisting schedule and more details on the listing of a Solana-based token.

In a recent post, Coinbase said trading for Stargate Finance (STG) and Vulcan Forged (PYR) has been delayed until November 20, 2026 at 2 PM ET. Trading of these tokens will be suspended on Coinbase (Simple and Advanced Trade), Coinbase Exchange and Coinbase Prime on this date.

Coinbase regularly reviews listed assets to make sure they meet required standards. In keeping with this and per recent reviews, Coinbase will halt trading for Ampleforth Governance Token (FORTH) on November 23, 2026 at 2 PM ET.

Also, according to recent reviews, Coinbase will delist Neon EVM (NEON) and Syndicate (SYND) on November 16, 2026 at 2 PM ET. Trading for these tokens will be suspended on Coinbase (Simple and Advanced Trade), Coinbase Exchange, and Coinbase Prime on this date, with users urged to act accordingly to prevent potential losses.

In recent listings, Solana-based token Collector Crypt (CARD) went live on the Coinbase platform and in its app, allowing Coinbase users to buy, sell, convert, send, receive, or store these assets.

In order to avoid a repeated ticker with an existing Coinbase listing, while the asset is natively known as CARDS, it will be traded under the ticker symbol CARD on the Coinbase platform and in the Coinbase iOS and Android apps.

This clarification remains essential to avoid confusion that may arise from token mix-ups, especially those with similar tickers that have been previously listed by Coinbase.

Coinbase reports that the conversion of Aerodrome Finance (AERO) and Velodrome Finance (VELO) to the new Aero (AERO) token is complete, with sends and receives for AERO and VELO re-enabled.

Coinbase no longer supports deposits or withdrawals of the legacy AERO token. Users are urged not to send the legacy AERO token to their Coinbase deposit address, as these deposits may not be recoverable.

Perpetual futures trading for BMNR, WDC, RKLB and AAOI will go live on Monday, November 9, 2026 at 10:00 AM UTC. Perpetual futures are available to retail traders and institutions on Coinbase, Coinbase Advanced, and Deribit in select jurisdictions.

#5258. Ether ETFs Face Another $99.9M Outflow as Demand Stays Uneven

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: US spot Ether ETFs posted a $99.9 million net outflow on November 5, Farside data show, following an $85.3 million outflow on November 3 and a temporary recovery on November 4. The pattern highlights a fragile demand picture, but cannot establish a direct forecast for ETH’s price.

Ether’s exchange-traded fund market is still searching for consistency. The latest fully reported daily figures in Farside Investors’ Ether ETF table show a $99.9 million net withdrawal on Thursday, November 5, just a day after the same group recorded a $53 million inflow. The reversal demonstrates why single-session data need context.

November 6’s table still contained unreported fields when reviewed, so it would be misleading to present the partial figure as a final market-wide total. A dated, complete session offers a better basis for an editorial assessment.

On November 5, BlackRock’s ETHA recorded a $75.2 million outflow and the firm’s ETHB line showed a further $22 million leaving. Fidelity’s FETH was down $2.2 million, while Grayscale’s ETHE showed a modest $500,000 withdrawal. Those figures add up to the $99.9 million negative session.

The previous day looked different. On November 4, the listed products collectively brought in $53 million, with positive flows in several funds offsetting negative readings elsewhere. Before that, November 2 and 3 brought losses of $139.7 million and $85.3 million respectively. The emerging picture is not a perfectly uninterrupted exodus. It is a market in which intermittent buying has yet to overcome repeated bouts of selling.

Our earlier report on November 2’s Ether ETF withdrawals provides a useful baseline. The subsequent sessions show that the pressure has persisted but has also varied substantially between days and issuers.

An ETF’s reported flow measures changes in the investment wrapper, not all Ethereum transactions. Institutions can obtain ETH exposure through spot holdings, derivatives, treasury companies and other vehicles. A negative day in the US listed fund category does not prove every sophisticated investor has turned bearish.

This is especially important for Ethereum because a significant share of the network’s economic activity occurs independently of an ETF. Stablecoin transfers, decentralised exchanges, tokenised assets and staking continue to operate whatever happens to a particular fund’s share count. The long-term investment case for ETH is often tied to that activity, but there is no automatic formula linking application usage to the token’s short-term price.

Concentration adds another layer. If one manager dominates the day’s net withdrawals, the aggregate should not be described as a consensus of all issuers. Analysts also need to separate price moves from flows: the dollar value of assets in a fund can fall because of market prices even when investors have not redeemed an equivalent amount of shares.

The first is whether ETF investors return in size and for more than a single session. The second is what happens in Ethereum’s development roadmap. The Ethereum Foundation announced a Glamsterdam testing delay on November 6, citing concerns about how proposed gas changes could affect existing applications. That is an engineering issue, not proof of a market-flow response, and it should be covered on its own terms.

For traders and longer-term holders, the practical takeaway is to compare several fully reported sessions, assess which issuers are driving the moves and resist treating flows as a stand-alone trading signal. Thursday’s number is meaningful evidence of weaker demand through US funds; it is not a reliable prediction of ETH’s next move.

#5257. SWIFT Debuts Own XRP-Inclusive Ledger With Dozen Banks

Home » Ripple (XRP) » SWIFT Debuts Own XRP-Inclusive Ledger With Dozen Banks

Ripple’s XRP extends connections to multiple banks in SWIFT’s blockchain-adopting line-up.

The biggest European financial conglomerate’s grandiose move to release their own blockchain ledger didn’t go unnoticed. This November as planned, the Society for Worldwide Interbank Financial Telecommunication (SWIFT) debuted the pilot version with 12 banks, some of which are familiar names in the XRP holder community.

One of them, HSBC, is slated to start blockchain-powered operations. HSBC connects its own Tokenised Deposit Service (TDS) to SWIFT’s ledger. The major British bank actively employs Metaco’s Harmonize platform for institutional digital-asset custody (Metaco was acquired by Ripple in 2023). The enterprise-level acquisition strengthened that bond.

5 OF SWIFT'S 17 LEDGER BANKS ALREADY WORK WITH RIPPLE 🚨Swift's new blockchain ledger is live with 17 banks piloting 24/7 cross-border payments.Look who's on that list:🏦 HSBC🏦 Standard Chartered🏦 DBS🏦 FirstRand🏦 BNY, which holds RLUSD's reserves and now holds XRP… https://t.co/z7UOp1KBYz pic.twitter.com/nNP5Ql4vgn

The bank also has historical references to XRPL capabilities in internal materials and some past explorations, with an agenda to expand blockchain-powered business operations. Conducting business operations on Distributed Ledger Technology (DLT) based chains like Ripple’s XRP Ledger serves an advantage of instant settlement & cost-effectiveness.

Aside from them, there’s a few more in the 12-long list. Standard Chartered, another popular British bank, has been running the XRP-related tech stack since 2016. During October 2026 the traditional finance behemoth launched their institutional custody for Ripple’s RLUSD stablecoin on the XRP Ledger through their Dubai (DIFC) branch.

Also, there’s DBS and BNY (BNY Mellon). These two Ripple-tied major banks serve completely different purposes. BNY Mellon is the primary reserve custodian for RLUSD. Meanwhile, DBS is partnered with Franklin Templeton to list a tokenized money-market fund (sgBENJI) on XRP Ledger, tradeable against RLUSD on their digital exchange.

🚨 ES OFICIALSWIFT acaba de confirmar transacciones en vivo en blockchain con más de 12 bancos.Y los reportes dicen que está impulsado por $XRP.Este es el tipo de noticia que la gente lleva años esperando.El sistema antiguo finalmente está siendo reemplazado en tiempo real?… pic.twitter.com/2uJi0VGPI9

This is a distinct product from the SWIFT project. SWIFT’s blockchain ledger is compatible with multiple DLT networks, but Ripple’s XRP Ledger has a crystal-clear advantage in regulatory compliance and native traits. XRP, along with Stellar’s XLM & Hedera’s HBAR has native ISO 20022 support, the new gold standard in financial messaging.

The cross-border payments market, worth over $155 trillion in processed volume annually, powers global trade, remittances & commerce. Yet merchants admit it remains slow, costly and not too efficient when compared to blockchain-based counterparts. 70% of merchants expect all transactions to settle immediately by 2027, which aligns with SWIFT’s vision.

SWIFT just named 30+ @Ripple connected banks in its new 2026 payments framework.HSBC, Deutsche Bank, Santander, Standard Chartered, JPMorgan… the list goes on.XRP isn’t replacing SWIFT. It’s becoming the liquidity bridge inside it. https://t.co/Y59ziIog9J

How much of that multi-trillion pie Ripple’s XRP can take is yet to be determined. On the SWIFT topic, Ripple’s CEO Brad Garlinghouse predicted a 14% capture. However, Ripple’s business ventures don’t always translate into blockchain adoption, as seen in the 2025 example. Ripple Prime, the custody arm, processed over $13 trillion without the help of crypto.

#5256. Stablecoins Go Mainstream as Samsung Wallet Adds USDC on Solana

Stablecoins Go Mainstream as Samsung Wallet Adds USDC on Solana

Samsung is putting digital dollars in the same app where its users keep their credit cards and boarding passes.

Stablecoin accounts went live in Samsung Wallet on Thursday for eligible U.S. Galaxy users, letting them buy, hold, send, and receive Circle’s USDC on the Solana blockchain without downloading a separate crypto app or managing private keys. The feature reaches about 82 million compatible Galaxy devices in the U.S., according to the announcement.

“For a decade, stablecoins have proved the technology works,” said Lily Liu, president of the Solana Foundation. “What they lacked was a way to reach the mainstream.”

Samsung first announced the feature on Oct. 7, when the electronics giant tapped Solana for USDC transfers. Samsung Wallet first teased plans to support the stablecoin, which is pegged to the U.S. dollar, over the summer.

The product is built with Bastion, a licensed stablecoin custodian and infrastructure provider, and Coinbase, which safeguards the USDC as Bastion’s sub-custodian through its Coinbase Prime Vault. Samsung’s disclosures stress that the account is a crypto wallet, not a bank account.

Users can send USDC to other Samsung Wallet users in the U.S. and to compatible outside crypto wallets with no Samsung transfer fee, though other providers may charge one. They can also send money to qualifying bank accounts in more than 60 countries, where recipients may receive local currency, with fees that vary by destination.

Topping up with a credit card, including through Samsung Pay, carries a 4% fee, and withdrawals to a bank account or debit card also come with a fee. New users who make a first top-up of $50 or more can receive $25 in USDC. The service requires Android 13 or later and is open to U.S. residents 18 and older, except those in New York.

"Stablecoins have the potential to make moving money around the world faster and easier, and we want Galaxy users to be able to take advantage of that without having to navigate the complexity of traditional crypto tools,” said Woncheol Chai, who leads the digital wallet team in Samsung’s mobile business. “Solana helps us bring that experience to Samsung's scale."

Samsung said it expects to explore online and in-store stablecoin payments and to expand to additional markets, subject to local regulation.

#5255. Why aren’t XLM crypto traders betting big before the 23 November vote?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Stellar’s [XLM] next upgrade just cleared its testnet vote. Validators will decide on the mainnet version in a couple of weeks.

Hence, the question – Has the token’s price moved in lockstep?

Bell (also known as Protocol 30), cleared its testnet vote on 3rd November. Validators will vote on the mainnet version on the 23rd.

According to the Stellar Development Foundation, Bell would bring three changes. The first will involve letting smart contract tell payments for different customers apart. This would make life immensely easier for businesses.

The second change will allow smart contracts check post-quantum signatures as the network’s own signatures would stay the same. The last would switch ledger timing to milliseconds. Here, it’s worth noting that they’ve admitted that this change along won’t make blocks faster.

Also, 23rd is not the final launch date. In fact, Bell only goes live if validators approve it, with the Foundation clarifying that the date could still move.

In the meantime, wallets, exchanges and node operators need to update their software.

Ideally, an upgrade would put the price up on its shoulders. Not this one though.

XLM went up to about $0.171 on the 4th, then came tumbling down the next afternoon. It’s been stuck between $0.153 and $0.157 since. At press time, it was valued at around $0.155.

Indicators seemed to be indecisive. The RSI was at 45.2, under the Signal Line. The ADX on the DMI was at around 18.5. What did this indicate? Well, there didn’t seem to be a strong trend either way, though sellers were just slightly ahead.

Futures traders have mostly taken a few steps back too. Open interest dropped from about $94 million to $88 million during the slide and at the time of writing, it was $89.5 million. Funding was barely positive at 0.0011% too.

Simply put, it may be evident that everyone’s playing it safe. No one’s betting big before the big 23rd November vote. For now, $0.16 is the level to watch.

#5254. Strive Reports $509M Quarterly Profit as Bitcoin Buying Accelerates

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Strive reported $509 million in third-quarter GAAP net income and said it purchased 8,136 BTC during the quarter, followed by another 15,124 BTC between October 1 and October 30. Its financing structure and accounting gains matter as much as the headline accumulation figures.

Bitcoin treasury company Strive has released a new set of numbers showing how rapidly its balance sheet has changed. In its November 6 third-quarter results, the Nasdaq-listed business reported $509 million in GAAP net income for the three months to September 30, alongside a large expansion of its Bitcoin position.

Strive said it acquired 8,136 BTC during the quarter and 20,373 BTC over the first nine months of 2026. It then bought another 15,124 BTC from October 1 through October 30. These are company-reported acquisition numbers, not an independent estimate of the market’s available Bitcoin supply.

The company reported $490.4 million in cash and cash equivalents as of October 30, plus a $50.1 million fair-value position in Strategy’s STRC preferred shares. It also closed an offering of seven million SATS preferred shares on October 20, reporting roughly $609 million in net proceeds after underwriting costs.

These details change how investors should interpret the Bitcoin buying. Purchasing more coins is easy to describe. The harder question is how those purchases are financed and how the resulting returns and risks are distributed among common and preferred shareholders. Preferred securities can provide capital, but they also introduce terms and obligations that differ from simply holding Bitcoin in a wallet.

That makes direct comparisons between treasury businesses difficult. NewsBTC recently reported Strategy’s purchase of another 811 BTC, a transaction accompanied by its own capital-management decisions. The underlying asset may be the same, but the capital structures are not.

Strive’s GAAP profit is an accounting figure reflecting the company’s reported results under applicable rules. Bitcoin price changes can create substantial gains or losses in reported earnings without corresponding sales or cash receipts. Investors need to inspect the financial statements and reconciliations rather than assume a nine-figure profit represents operating cash generated from customers.

The company also disclosed a third-quarter Bitcoin yield of 18.5% and a year-to-date yield of 63.2%. Those are management-defined measures intended to assess Bitcoin growth relative to a particular share denominator. They are not conventional investment yields, cash dividends or guaranteed returns. As the share count and preferred capital change, the denominator matters enormously.

Strive said it was evaluating additional balance-sheet strategies spanning lending, brokerages, decentralised finance, reinsurance and tokenisation. Evaluating an opportunity is not the same as launching a product. Each area carries regulatory, counterparty or execution risks that could complicate an otherwise straightforward treasury narrative.

Three things deserve attention: updated fully diluted share counts, the costs and terms of preferred financing, and the company’s actual liquidity alongside its Bitcoin exposure. A rising coin balance is not sufficient evidence that every existing shareholder’s economic interest in those coins has increased.

The company’s November 6 release provides a useful dated snapshot rather than an assurance of future performance. NewsBTC’s reporting on the broader Bitcoin hashrate cycle is a reminder that the coin’s market and infrastructure operate beyond any one corporation’s strategy. For now, Strive has demonstrated aggressive accumulation. Whether it creates durable value for common shareholders will depend on capital discipline as much as on Bitcoin itself.

#5253. Ripple Veteran David Schwartz Draws the Line With XRP Spammers

Ripple CTO Emeritus and renowned XRP legend, David Schwartz has expressed displeasure while issuing a strict warning to members of the XRP community over growing conduct he finds unacceptable.

In a post shared earlier today, Schwartz revealed plans to block most XRP users over the growing number of irrelevant replies and unsolicited promotions recently flooding his posts on X.

Schwartz's warning came after he explained that he has recently noticed a growing pattern of irrelevant crypto-related comments on his posts on X.

He mentioned that there has been a substantial increase in users, especially from the XRP community, responding to his comments on unrelated posts with insults, accusations, and crypto-related messages.

According to him, the behavior has become disruptive enough that he now has to block some of those accounts, as he explained that the replies not only affect his interactions but also inconvenience other X users whose posts he engages with.

Schwartz considers this conduct very concerning, as flooding unrelated conversations with XRP content or personal attacks can force the people he is responding to to block him to prevent their followers from receiving irrelevant notifications.

In his post, Schwartz further spotlighted the increasing fraud allegations he has continued to receive on his unrelated posts, revealing plans to take action on that as well.

He mentioned that he will block the accounts of users who follow him across X to accuse him of fraud or scamming, whether their comments are positive or negative.

While expressing his displeasure, Schwartz warned that he would not tolerate people using his participation in other users' discussions as an opportunity to promote XRP or attack him.

#5252. Bitcoin ETFs could surpass mutual funds in AUM by 2028: Bloomberg analyst

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

As we are about to enter the third year of the Spot Bitcoin [BTC] ETF being launched, it appears that BTC ETFs have a significantly stronger influence on BTC price returns than corporate Bitcoin accumulation.

According to the latest analysis by Ecoinometrics, an inflow of 10,000 BTC into spot Bitcoin ETFs corresponds to an estimated 3.2% boost in monthly Bitcoin returns. In comparison, the same amount of Bitcoin purchased by public companies contributes approximately 0.7% to monthly returns.

This suggests that ETF purchases have roughly 4.5 times the impact of corporate buying on Bitcoin’s price performance.

Adding more to this analysis, Bloomberg’s James Seyffart suggests that ETFs could surpass mutual funds in total Assets Under Management (AUM) within the next two to three years.

Their growth is only accelerating. If we stay on the same trend we’ve been on since the summer it will happen in ~two years.

Confirming his prediction, Syeffart used a chart and highlighted that ETFs accounted for 40.21% of total fund assets as of September 2026. And given the recent growth trends, ETFs could reach the 50% mark as early as September 2028 if the three-month growth rate continues.

However, other projections estimate this milestone between November 2028 and December 2029, depending on the period considered.

At the same time, ETFs rolling 12-month growth rate have also reached 3.74% in September 2026, above the historical average of 2.78% since the 2020 COVID market bottom. This indicates that ETF adoption is expanding faster than its long-term average.

This comes as BTC ETFs are in their outflows streak. According to Farisde Investors data, from 1st to 5th November 2026, Bitcoin ETFs saw mostly outflows. In fact, so far in November, it has already recorded $869.82 million in outflows. The only exception being on the 4th of November, when the BTC ETFs recorded a net inflow of $74.6 million.

All of this happened as the price of BTC was trading at $76,102.46 after a drop of 52% in the past 24 hours.

That said, besides Bitcoin ETFs, Ethereum [ETH] ETFs also recorded $272.09  in outflows, and Binance [BNB] ETFs recorded $271.01K in outflows. Meanwhile, XRP ETFs were funded by $529.51K in outflows, Solana [SOL] ETFs had $1.18M in outflows, and Hyperliquid [HYPE] ETFs also saw $7.66 million in outflows.

In short, the pattern suggests risk-off sentiment across crypto at the start of November, and not just in Bitcoin. So, given the current data, it remains to be seen whether Seyffart’s predictions turn into reality or not.

#5251. BitFuFu’s October Bitcoin Mining Output Falls Despite Steady Hashrate

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: BitFuFu produced 137 BTC in October, down from 167 in September, while managed hashrate stayed close to 19.5 EH/s. The miner attributed the decline partly to rising network difficulty, illustrating why steady capacity does not necessarily mean steady production.

A Bitcoin miner can run broadly the same amount of computing power and still produce fewer coins. BitFuFu’s latest monthly operating statement provides a neat example. The company reported November 6 that it mined 137 BTC in October, down from 167 in September, even as total hashrate under management remained largely unchanged.

Its managed rate stood at 19.5 exahashes per second, compared with 19.4 EH/s a month earlier. The company held 1,454 BTC at October’s end, up from 1,388 in September. Production decreased while the treasury balance increased, because production and coin retention are separate measures.

BitFuFu said Bitcoin network difficulty rose about 11.8% during October, from 132.76 trillion at the beginning of the month to 148.36 trillion at month-end. Difficulty adjusts how hard it is for miners collectively to find valid blocks. When competition increases, a miner with an unchanged share of computing power may capture a smaller share of the rewards than before.

The split inside BitFuFu’s operation was also revealing. Self-mining production fell to 67 BTC from 98 BTC, while cloud-mining production was nearly flat at 70 BTC against 69 BTC in September. Total monthly production therefore declined by 30 BTC, but the effect was not identical across its operating categories.

NewsBTC recently examined Bitcoin’s recovery above one zettahash of network hashrate. That broader competition can be healthy for network security while making mining economics more demanding for individual operators. It is possible for both statements to be true at once.

BitFuFu’s average fleet efficiency improved to 16.3 joules per terahash from 16.7 in September. Lower energy use per unit of hashing is useful, although it does not automatically offset higher difficulty, power prices, financing costs or changes in Bitcoin’s market price. The company also reported power capacity under management of 317 megawatts, compared with 324 MW a month earlier.

These figures are particularly relevant when evaluating mining businesses that sell both direct Bitcoin exposure and computing services. Managed hashrate can include machines owned by clients or supplied by third parties, not just equipment on the company’s balance sheet. Investors should therefore distinguish self-owned hashrate, contractual capacity, energy efficiency and coins actually received.

A miner accumulating coins can improve its Bitcoin exposure even while production slows. But treasury growth alone says little about the cost of producing each BTC or the cash needed to keep equipment operating. The real test is whether operations remain resilient when difficulty rises and the market price fails to keep pace.

The next report will show whether October’s production weakness was temporary or persisted under the next difficulty environment. Useful metrics include self-mined output, third-party mining contribution, installed and energised capacity, average fleet efficiency and the amount of Bitcoin retained rather than sold.

BitFuFu’s figures are unaudited operating metrics, not a full measure of profitability. They nevertheless make the central point clearly: Bitcoin mining is a competitive process, and an operator can improve its hardware efficiency while producing fewer coins. That is why mining comparisons need more than a headline hashrate number.

#5249. Morning Minute: Democrat Sweep Pumps Crypto as Clarity Act Resurfaces

Morning Minute: Democrat Sweep Pumps Crypto as Clarity Act Resurfaces

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🗳️ Democrat Sweep Pumps Crypto as Market Structure Bill Resurfaces

Bitcoin spent Tuesday night under $76,000, a seven-week low, with traders bracing for whatever a Democratic Congress meant for crypto. By Wednesday afternoon it was back above $80,800, up 4% on the day.

The easy story is that uncertainty lifted and crypto exhaled. And that could be part of it. But oil helped. WTI fell 9% to $88 a barrel, down almost 17% on the week, as Iran’s parliament speaker said voters had “said NO TO WAR.” The DOW jumped 3% on the day and Nasdaq +1.6% in what was a huge green day across markets.

James Butterfill of CoinShares put it plainly: “For Bitcoin, real yields, dollar liquidity and ETP flows still carry more weight than the elections.”

But the policy news that landed Wednesday afternoon matters more than the candles.

Eleanor Terrett reported that House Democrats are discussing expanding Maxine Waters’ Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights, originally an amendment to the CLARITY Act in 2025, into a full market structure bill next Congress. The draft would fold in Democratic CLARITY provisions that the House rejected last year, plus provisions Senate Democrats and Republicans had already agreed on before the September cloture vote that killed the bill. Three industry sources described the talks to her.

That’s a genuine surprise. Twenty-four hours ago the consensus was that market structure legislation was dead for two years. Waters is the likely incoming chair of House Financial Services, and she is apparently planning to write a bill rather than hold hearings. But the bill will have some changes.

The new proposals under discussion would give the SEC a larger role supervising crypto markets and leave the CFTC with a smaller one. The draft is expected to carry ethics provisions restricting the crypto activities of Trump and other federal officials. Those provisions are what Democrats wanted all along, and what many thought would kill any chances the bill has of getting signed.

So one of the first courses of action from this Democrat-led Congress is to enact some reasonable changes to the Clarity Act to try to get it passed in 2027. That feels like a very bullish first step.

#5247. Ethereum Delays Glamsterdam Testnet Step Over Gas-Accounting Risks

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Ethereum developers have postponed the next Glamsterdam hard-fork step on the Hoodi testnet after researchers identified applications that may depend on gas-accounting assumptions the upgrade would change. The Ethereum Foundation is asking teams to audit code before the process advances.

Ethereum’s next major protocol upgrade has hit an important compatibility check. In a November 6 update, Ethereum Foundation developer Marius van der Wijden said the Glamsterdam testing process would not proceed to the Hoodi fork until problems identified during testing were resolved.

The concern is not that the Ethereum network has stopped functioning. Rather, some existing applications could break because they rely on assumptions about how gas is charged. The Foundation identified EIP-8037 and EIP-8038 as the proposals that require closer scrutiny.

Ethereum gas is commonly understood as the cost of executing transactions and smart-contract operations. Developers also use gas-related properties inside application design and testing. If the protocol alters the accounting model, code built around old assumptions can behave differently even when its business logic has not been edited.

The Foundation said ChainSecurity had found projects that could be vulnerable after Glamsterdam because they depend on invariants that the proposed changes would break. An invariant is a condition developers assume will continue to hold. Breaking one does not automatically mean every affected contract can be exploited, but it can introduce incorrect behaviour, unexpected failures or security exposure.

The engineering team is working with ChainSecurity and Dedaub, alongside internal auditing efforts. It has also published a repository describing eight relevant invariants and examples that developers can check. This is a call for affected application teams to participate in the review, not merely an internal protocol discussion.

Blockchain upgrades have a difficult obligation: improve the underlying platform without unexpectedly damaging applications already deployed on it. The network is open, and its developers cannot inspect every third-party contract, off-chain integration or custom execution path. Testnets and audits are designed to identify precisely this kind of problem before users face it in production.

The Foundation said AllCoreDevs decided not to fork Hoodi until the issues were resolved, while work on the later Hegota upgrade would continue in parallel. That separation is useful. It suggests the immediate bottleneck sits in the Glamsterdam compatibility review rather than a blanket suspension of all Ethereum development.

For operators, the responsible response is practical rather than dramatic. Teams should check whether their contracts or surrounding code depend on the identified gas assumptions, test against the updated specifications and document any affected flows. Even a contract that appears unchanged could be involved if another component makes assumptions about its gas use.

The November 6 post did not establish a new final deployment date, so assigning one would be speculation. The meaningful milestones are revised specifications if necessary, confirmed remediation of affected cases, a successful Hoodi test and clear guidance for applications that may need modifications.

Ethereum’s wider activity continues independently of this testing decision. NewsBTC has recently tracked ETF flows, but fund demand and protocol engineering should not be collapsed into one cause-and-effect story. Here, the verifiable development is a cautious pause after discovering compatibility risk. For a network hosting substantial financial activity, finding that risk before deployment is the critical distinction.

This article was written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

#5245. Best Crypto-Friendly Banks: 5 Options for Crypto Investors and Businesses

Home » Cryptopedia » Best Crypto-Friendly Banks: 5 Options for Crypto Investors and Businesses

Which banks will support your crypto habits?

Finding a crypto-friendly bank in 2026 is not as simple as opening an account with a traditional financial institution. Some banks allow transfers to cryptocurrency exchanges like Coinbase, while others offer built-in crypto trading, digital asset custody, or specialized banking services for Web3 businesses.

The best option depends on where you live, whether you are an individual investor or a crypto business, and which digital asset services you need. Some providers also impose restrictions on specific exchanges, transaction limits, or crypto-related business activities.

In this guide, we compare crypto-friendly banks and fintech providers, examining their supported services, availability, fees, and limitations to help you find the right banking solution in 2026.

A crypto-friendly bank makes it easier for individuals and businesses to interact with the cryptocurrency market through traditional financial services. Depending on the provider, this may include allowing transfers to and from crypto exchanges, offering crypto trading or payment features, or providing banking services to eligible crypto companies.

Not all crypto-friendly providers offer the same features. Some let customers buy, sell, and hold digital assets directly, while others simply support transfers to selected platforms like Kraken or provide financial services to Web3 businesses. Some are licensed banks, while others are fintech companies that partner with banks to deliver their services.

Before exploring the best crypto-friendly banks, consider the main advantages and potential drawbacks.

If you’re looking for the best crypto-friendly bank to join in 2023, you’re in the right place! This list breaks down some of the top banks for cryptocurrency from a variety of fields, including traditional banking, business banking, and personal accounts.

Revolut remains one of the more accessible options for people who want to manage traditional money and cryptocurrency through a single financial app.

Revolut is a digital banking platform that combines traditional financial services with cryptocurrency features. In supported markets, customers can use its app to buy, sell, hold, and transfer cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin and Ethereum. Available assets and features depend on the customer’s country and account eligibility.

Revolut also supports crypto withdrawals and deposits through selected external wallets and blockchain networks. However, transaction fees, supported assets, and withdrawal limits vary. Its cryptocurrency services are provided by separate legal entities from its banking services, and crypto holdings are not covered by standard bank deposit protection.

Wirex flips the script amongst the other ‘best crypto-friendly banks’ contenders. While other platforms began their story as traditional banks and slowly moved to cryptocurrency acceptance, Wirex set out to be the world’s leading crypto bank from day one.

Wirex is a global fintech platform combining traditional currencies, cryptocurrency services, and payment cards. Users in eligible markets can buy, sell, exchange, and hold supported digital assets, as well as use available payment services to spend their funds.

Wirex also hosts a cross-chain bridge, offers crypto-linked cards, and a Cryptoback rewards programme, which advertises rewards of up to 8% on eligible purchases.

Actual rewards depend on the card programme, subscription, and applicable conditions. Wirex is not a traditional bank; its services are provided through regulated entities and financial partners, depending on the customer’s location.

Mercury is a US-based financial technology company providing business banking services through partner banks. It is particularly relevant to eligible cryptocurrency and Web3 startups, DAOs, and investment funds that need traditional financial infrastructure to manage their operations.

Businesses can use Mercury to manage fiat deposits, payments, expenses, and transfers to supported cryptocurrency exchanges, including Coinbase and Gemini. However, Mercury does not hold cryptocurrencies or provide crypto trading services.

It supports most eligible US-registered businesses, including those founded by international entrepreneurs, but does not accept applications from crypto exchanges or money services businesses.

Monzo is a UK-based digital bank that allows customers to use their accounts with selected cryptocurrency exchanges.

Although it does not offer native cryptocurrency trading, customers can transfer money to supported platforms, including Coinbase, making it an option for investors who want to manage traditional banking and crypto purchases separately.

Monzo applies transaction monitoring and restrictions to cryptocurrency-related payments. For example, it currently blocks payments to Binance while allowing transfers to a range of other exchanges. Its cryptocurrency payment allowance is also limited, so customers should check the latest terms before making large transactions.

Ally Bank is a US-based digital bank offering traditional banking products, including checking accounts, savings accounts, and investment services. Unlike specialist crypto-friendly financial platforms, Ally does not provide direct cryptocurrency trading or cryptocurrency custody through its banking services.

Customers cannot transfer cryptocurrencies directly to Ally accounts, which accept transactions in US dollars. Ally Invest also does not currently support direct cryptocurrency trading, although customers can access certain crypto-related investment products, including cryptocurrency funds and publicly traded companies involved in the industry.

Ally is therefore better suited to customers looking for conventional banking and investment services rather than direct access to cryptocurrency exchanges or digital assets.

Choosing a crypto-friendly bank involves more than finding a financial institution that allows cryptocurrency transactions. The right option depends on your location, investment activities, and whether you need basic exchange transfers or more advanced crypto-related services.

Here are five important factors to consider:

Check whether the bank supports transfers to your preferred cryptocurrency exchanges, such as Coinbase, Kraken, or Binance. Some financial institutions restrict payments to specific platforms or apply additional security checks to crypto-related transactions.

Compare account maintenance fees, international transfer costs, currency conversion charges, and any limits on cryptocurrency-related payments. Some providers also charge separate fees for buying, selling, or withdrawing digital assets.

Look for providers with appropriate regulatory authorisation and transparent security policies. Check whether eligible fiat deposits are covered by a recognised deposit guarantee scheme and understand that cryptocurrency holdings generally receive different protections.

Crypto-related banking services are not available everywhere. Before opening an account, check whether the provider accepts customers in your country and whether its cryptocurrency features are accessible in your region.

If you operate a Web3 startup, blockchain company, or cryptocurrency-related business, check the provider’s business eligibility requirements. Some platforms, including Mercury, support eligible technology and Web3 companies but exclude certain activities, such as operating a cryptocurrency exchange or money services business.

Crypto-friendly banks and financial platforms make it easier to manage traditional money and cryptocurrency in one place. However, their safety depends on regulation, banking partnerships, and security measures.

An important distinction is that eligible fiat deposits may be protected by deposit guarantee schemes, while cryptocurrencies generally are not. Crypto assets also carry risks such as market volatility, custody issues, and potential losses.

Not every crypto-friendly platform is a licensed bank. Providers such as Wirex and Mercury may operate through regulated entities or partner banks. Before choosing one, check its licences, account terms, and what protections actually apply to your funds.

Several financial institutions and fintech platforms support cryptocurrency-related transactions. Revolut and Wirex offer crypto services in eligible markets, while Monzo supports transfers to selected cryptocurrency exchanges. Mercury provides banking services to eligible Web3 businesses, whereas Ally Bank primarily offers traditional banking and investment products.

The best option depends on your needs. Revolut is suitable for individuals seeking integrated crypto services, Wirex focuses on cryptocurrency payments and spending, and Mercury caters to eligible Web3 businesses. Monzo can be useful for UK customers transferring money to supported exchanges.

Yes, many banks and financial platforms allow transfers to Coinbase, although availability depends on the customer’s location, payment method, and account restrictions. Always check your bank’s current cryptocurrency payment policy and Coinbase’s supported funding methods before initiating a transfer.

Some institutions also block transactions involving specific exchanges. Customers should review their provider’s terms and conditions before making crypto-related transfers.

Eligible deposits held with licensed banks may be covered by national deposit protection schemes, depending on the institution, account type, and applicable limits. However, cryptocurrency holdings are generally not covered by traditional bank deposit insurance. Fintech platforms may also use partner banks, so customers should identify the institution actually holding their deposits and verify the applicable protection.

#5244. Sam Altman Says It's 'Fucking Annoying' His RAM Comments Were Taken Out of Context

Sam Altman Says It's 'Fucking Annoying' His RAM Comments Were Taken Out of Context

OpenAI CEO Sam Altman has hit back at criticism of his comments about computer memory, saying they were taken out of context and that he has long argued for the world to make more of it.

"I actually do want the world to have tons of memory and people to put as much as they want in their computers, and it is fucking annoying that this was taken out of context," Altman tweeted early Wednesday. "I have been arguing for the world to ramp up memory production for a long time."

but only because...i actually do want the world to have tons of memory and people to put as much as they want in their computers, and it is fucking annoying that this was taken out of context.

i have been arguing for the world to ramp up memory production for a long time.

The post came as Altman leaned into the backlash. Replying to users joking about his remarks, he said he "lol'd" at one meme and, when another asked him to rename his X account "Ram Altman," replied "ok yeah," briefly changing his display name before explaining he had only done so because he wanted people to have plenty of memory.

Altman's original comments came in an interview with YouTube channel Cheeky Pint, where he discussed shortages of memory and other components caused by the AI boom. "From like a global allocation perspective, actually, it's quite terrible to have everybody have a bunch of the world's limited supply of DRAM sitting in their own computer and being used 5% of the time," he said. "You want maximum utilization in a cloud if you're trying to deal with this very supply-constrained environment."

In the same interview, Altman also blamed memory makers for being slow to expand production, and said he would be "very sad" if prices for chips and other components did not fall.

The remarks drew widespread criticism, including from Elon Musk, who mocked Altman on Sunday with a meme of the Inglourious Basterds character Hans Landa asking, "You are withholding DRAM from the cloud, are you not?" "The people deserve to have their RAM too!" Musk wrote.

The spat comes amid a global memory shortage driven by demand from AI data centers. SK hynix, one of the world's largest memory makers, warned last week that demand for memory would outstrip supply through 2030.

Contract prices for 16GB DDR5 memory chips rose from $8.70 in October 2025 to $48 in September 2026, an increase of more than 550%, according to Tom's Hardware. On an earnings call this week, Chen Ching-wen, CEO of memory maker Team Group, said there was "no downward price trend whatsoever," and warned that some buyers could face a situation where "even money can't buy goods."

The crunch has already pushed up prices for consumer devices. In April, Meta raised the price of its Quest 3 and Quest 3S headsets by $50 to $100, weeks after Sony increased PlayStation 5 prices.

#5243. Samsung Wallet’s USDC on Solana Is Live — Here’s What the Rollout Actually Covers

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: A Solana Foundation update dated November 5 confirms that Samsung Wallet’s stablecoin account went live on November 2 for eligible US Galaxy owners. USDC transfers and certain international bank payouts are supported, but the headline 82 million compatible devices should not be mistaken for 82 million active users.

A crypto feature that does not feel like a crypto feature may be one of the most consequential ways digital payments reach a wider audience. In a November 5 launch confirmation, the Solana Foundation said Samsung Wallet’s Stablecoin Account became available on November 2. For eligible US Galaxy users, it places USDC inside a familiar wallet app rather than asking them to install and learn a separate cryptocurrency application.

That is a tangible step beyond the launch window described in NewsBTC’s earlier look at Samsung’s planned rollout. But the most useful questions concern what people can do today, who qualifies and what still depends on outside providers.

The Foundation says eligible users can buy, hold, send and receive USDC over Solana within Samsung Wallet. Bastion provides licensed custody and infrastructure, with Coinbase serving as sub-custodian through Coinbase Prime Vault. That structure matters. Customers see the convenience of a familiar interface, while their assets still depend on a chain of infrastructure and custody providers.

US customers can transfer USDC to other eligible Samsung Wallet users and compatible external wallets. The announcement says Samsung charges no transfer fee for these routes, although other providers may charge fees. A separate path allows eligible users to send funds to qualifying bank accounts in more than 60 countries, potentially arriving as local currency. Available corridors and charges vary by destination; the distinction between a blockchain transfer and a bank payout is important.

The feature sits inside Samsung Wallet’s Quick access area alongside payment cards and everyday credentials. That small interface decision could matter more to adoption than a headline transaction-speed benchmark, because habitual use often depends on how much friction an app removes.

The announcement refers to approximately 82 million compatible Galaxy devices in the United States. That is a ceiling for potential access, not evidence of adoption. It says nothing about the number of activated accounts, transfers completed, average transaction size or users who return the following month.

The eligibility restrictions are also substantial. According to the Foundation, users need to be at least 18, reside in the US, have a Samsung account and use a compatible Galaxy device running Android 13 or later. New York residents are excluded at launch. These requirements make the real reachable population smaller than the device figure.

The payments story extends the institutional work covered in NewsBTC’s report on Solana’s delivery-versus-payment tool, but the two solve different problems. One targets consumer remittances; the other aims at financial-asset settlement.

Samsung has described potential future expansion into online and in-store stablecoin payments and additional markets, subject to regulation. Those ideas are not live features simply because the stablecoin account has launched. Nor should this launch be read as a forecast for SOL’s price: consumer payments can be valuable without creating a predictable relationship between wallet activity and demand for the network token.

A fair assessment will require active-account numbers, real-world fees, payout times, failed-payment handling and whether the feature reduces friction relative to existing remittance services. The verified news is that the service is now operational for a defined group of customers. The commercial test begins after that announcement, when people decide whether to use it again.

Known for his constant criticism of Bitcoin and its major holders, especially Michael Saylor, Schiff has mocked Strategy following Bitcoin's severe price crash recently.

Following his assertions, Schiff further introduced his long-standing Bitcoin vs. Gold argument, suggesting that Saylor made the wrong choice of asset for his investments.

He argued that investing in gold instead would have doubled Saylor's money over the same period, triggering fresh debates across the crypto ecosystem.

Schiff's statement does not come as a surprise, as he is publicly known for repeatedly questioning Bitcoin's potential as a long-term investment.

The sworn Bitcoin critic has often argued that precious metals, especially gold, offer a more reliable store of value than Bitcoin, consistently describing Bitcoin as a Ponzi scheme.

#5241. Tom Lee says Ethereum is bottoming – A whale is betting $45M that he’s right

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Volatility often goes hand in hand with speculation, and Ethereum is a clear example of this.

The past 48 hours have been a rollercoaster for the altcoin. According to CoinGlass, nearly $300 million in long positions have been wiped out.

However, this still does not compare to the liquidity squeeze seen earlier in October, which saw almost $500 million of positions liquidated as ETH broke below the $2.5k support level, triggering a 7% correction.

This time, despite erasing nearly $300 million worth of long positions, ETH has corrected by just over 1%. This suggests that selling pressure remains relatively contained, at least for now. Could this mean Tom Lee was right to say Ethereum is close to bottoming out?

As can be seen in the post above, a trader who claims to have a perfect 100% win rate has opened up another long trade on Ethereum. The trader had used 25x leverage to go long on 20,277 ETH for an investment of $45.3 million.

This trade has already resulted in $252,000 of profit. The interesting part is that the trader’s  previous 10 trades have all been profitable, resulting in $6.32 million in realized gains.

Given this track record and the fact that ETH has had limited downside despite heavy futures selling, this could be an interesting sign.

It could suggest that the trader is positioning ahead of a potential recovery that the broader market has yet to price in, adding another bullish angle to Tom Lee’s $2k ETH bottom thesis.

That said, the real test may still be ahead.

The market is still not fully convinced that Ethereum [ETH] has found its bottom.

For starters, on-chain data suggests that uncertainty is also showing up in staking demand, which continues to weaken. According to ValidatorQueue, there are about 1,036,882 ETH (over $2.3 billion) waiting to go into the Ethereum mainnet validator queue.

This is a considerable drop compared to 3,737,802 ETH six months ago, suggesting fewer investors are looking to lock up their ETH for staking rewards.

Meanwhile, institutional sentiment remains negative. Over the past five weeks, there have been continuous outflows from Ethereum ETFs, with $270 million in net outflows already recorded in less than a week since November began.

If this trend continues, November could see outflows exceed October’s $1.6 billion, putting further pressure on ETH and its recovery prospects.

Unless these factors start to improve, calling an Ethereum bottom could be premature.

For now, ETH remains exposed to further downside. Long positions are building despite fading technicals, weakening interest in staking demand, and increased ETF outflows.

On top of this, a large long-liquidation cluster exists below $2200 ETH. If overall sentiment remains weak, ETH could sweep this liquidity, potentially falling below the $2k level before a potential reversal.

This suggests that the market may need another round of downside before Tom Lee’s bottom thesis gets stronger support.

A big Ethereum trader is betting on a recovery despite recent market volatility.

Falling staking demand, ETF outflows, and liquidation risks could push ETH below $2,000.

#5240. Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Plunge, $428 Million in Crypto Longs Get Wiped Out

Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Plunge, $428 Million in Crypto Longs Get Wiped Out

Bitcoin erased Wednesday's post-election rebound Thursday as a technology stock selloff spread to cryptocurrencies, triggering hundreds of millions in forced liquidations.

Bitcoin fell to about $76,500, down nearly 5% and back near levels seen before Wednesday's jump above $80,000. Ethereum shed more than 5%, while other major tokens suffered comparable losses. Roughly 90% of the top 100 cryptocurrencies were in the red during the selloff, while roughly $428 million in crypto longs were liquidated in one hour.

Wall Street offered no shelter. The S&P 500 lost about 2.6% in late-morning trading, while the Nasdaq sank nearly 4%, approaching a correction, or a 10% drop from its peak.

The election is part of the answer, but anxiety over AI spending and interest rates is making the damage worse.

Democrats' sweep of Congress initially rattled investors expecting tougher oversight of industries favored by President Donald Trump. Stocks rallied Wednesday as traders bet gridlock would limit major policy changes and falling oil prices would ease inflation. Bitwise Europe analyst André Dragosch also told Decrypt the Democratic sweep was "largely priced in."

Thursday brought a different interpretation. Investors weighed what Democratic control might mean for the data-center boom, particularly in states where voters oppose rising electricity costs and new facilities.

And the new political configuration is already moving things. Voters in at least 29 U.S. jurisdictions backed restrictions on data-center development, although not every measure is binding. UBS estimates potential pauses could threaten 30% of planned U.S. data-center capacity additions in 2028, per Reuters.

"We suspect there is going to be some scrutiny on this aggressive AI buildout," Swissquote senior analyst Ipek Ozkardeskaya told Reuters.

Chipmakers and AI infrastructure companies took the hit first. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index dropped about 8%, while CoreWeave and Nebius fell by double digits. Tesla also tumbled roughly 9.5%, extending losses after Elon Musk spent heavily backing Republican candidates. Investors were also questioning the returns on massive AI investments.

The S&P 500 had the biggest bearish intraday movement in months, dropping almost 4% in minutes.

"The market's moving from the promise of AI to the price of AI," Ted Parkhill, chief executive of Incline Investment Management, told Reuters.

The political shift also threatens crypto's ambitions in Washington. Democratic control complicates efforts to revive the stalled Clarity Act, a proposed federal rulebook for digital assets. The new Congress will not take office until January. Politics alone cannot explain Bitcoin losing thousands of dollars in an hour.

Wednesday's rally looked dramatic, but much of its buying pressure came from traders closing losing bets against Bitcoin. About $378 million in short positions were liquidated in one hour as Bitcoin pushed above $80,000, according to CoinGlass data.

Today’s reversal flipped the liquidation machinery in the opposite direction. Exchanges automatically close underfunded longs as prices fall, generating more selling and the enormous red candles visible on Bitcoin's hourly chart.

Crypto was already vulnerable before the election. The market had lost roughly $300 billion in value over the previous month, amid concerns about AI spending, rising borrowing costs and the fallout from last week's multibillion-dollar Binance hack.

Traders also expect another Federal Reserve rate increase in December as inflation persists. Friday's October jobs report could alter those bets; higher rates generally hurt demand for speculative investments.

Technically, Bitcoin's still-forming daily candle is threatening a bearish engulfing pattern, potentially swallowing Wednesday's green body and erasing the breakout in a single session.

The hourly chart shows selling through $79,000 to near $76,100. The next test is $75,000, while reclaiming $80,000 could ease the pressure—but Thursday's candle has not closed, so the pattern remains unconfirmed.

#5239. Solana’s October Upgrade Review Puts 200ms Slots and $5B in Tokenized Assets in Focus

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Solana Foundation’s November 6 ecosystem review says two protocol changes went live in October: a reduction in target slot time to 200 milliseconds and Alpenglow consensus activation. It also reports a record $5 billion in real-world asset value. These are different metrics, and none alone proves a better experience for every application.

After a month filled with announcements, Solana has published a consolidated account of what changed on its network. The Foundation’s October 2026 ecosystem roundup, released November 6, links a pair of major performance upgrades with increased tokenized-asset activity and a growing roster of payment integrations.

The review is useful because it separates infrastructure that has activated from applications and partnerships still seeking wider use. It also offers a chance to examine how technical speed claims translate into financial operations.

According to the Foundation, a final stage of SIMD-0525 activated on October 9, reducing target slot times from the original 400 milliseconds to 200 milliseconds. Shorter slots aim to reduce the interval at which network leaders produce blocks. They do not guarantee that every transaction is confirmed within 200 milliseconds under all network conditions.

A second change followed on October 21 when Alpenglow replaced TowerBFT consensus on mainnet. The protocol targets roughly 150-millisecond finality. Finality describes when a transaction is sufficiently settled according to the network’s rules, and should not be confused with transaction submission, application response time or a merchant receiving funds in a bank account.

NewsBTC covered the Alpenglow activation earlier in the autumn. The new roundup does not make it a second launch. It provides a broader, dated view of how the two changes fit together, while reminding readers that production reliability has to be measured over time.

The Foundation reports that real-world assets on Solana reached $5 billion in value during October, alongside approximately $60 billion in decentralized-exchange trading volume and 14 million addresses holding stablecoins. These numbers measure different things. Asset value is not trading volume; a blockchain address is not necessarily a person; and gross turnover is not revenue to applications or validators.

The review includes new or expanded connections to traditional finance. J.P. Morgan Asset Management brought tokenized money market funds to the network through Morgan Money. The Foundation also describes stock-related products from Securitize and investment portfolios from Ondo, subject to their own eligibility conditions. For institutional users, the question is not only whether a token can move quickly but whether its legal ownership, redemption rights and custody arrangements remain clear.

That is also the theme behind Solana’s delivery-versus-payment programme, which seeks to coordinate an asset and its payment in one settlement process. Better throughput can help, but it cannot replace legal and operational safeguards.

The Foundation’s update lists stablecoin and remittance activity involving Samsung Wallet, Fiserv and Western Union, as well as integrations with business-payment services. Some products have launched, while other announcements refer to planned support, geographically limited access or commercial pilots. Those distinctions should survive any headline about ‘mainstream adoption’.

For developers and validators, the next meaningful evidence is sustained uptime, consistent finality under load, integration reliability and the cost of operating infrastructure. For investors, it is whether tokenized value corresponds to assets customers can actually redeem and use. Solana’s October was busy by the Foundation’s account; November’s challenge is showing the upgrades make routine financial activity more dependable, not merely more impressive on paper.

#5238. BNB Chain Launches 'Red Alarm' to Combat Rising Security Risks

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

BNB Chain has launched a new security feature, "Red Alarm," to help users identify potentially risky decentralized applications (dApps) and smart contracts in its ecosystem.

"Red Alarm" aims to improve user protection by highlighting potentially high-risk dApps and contracts, providing users with more information to consider before interacting with them.

BNB Chain said user safety in the crypto industry is a work in progress, especially as decentralized finance and blockchain applications are on the rise. As more users engage with smart contract systems and decentralized platforms, it has become increasingly important to identify potential security threats.

Keeping users safe takes ongoing work across crypto. BNB Chain’s Red Alarm contributes by flagging potentially high-risk dApps and contracts, giving users more information to work with. Explore the list 👇 https://t.co/FtWI1mWFVk https://t.co/hiTEVKyTyn

Through Red Alarm, users can access security-related warnings that may help them recognize suspicious applications and contracts before committing funds or approving transactions.

This measure follows after the Binance crypto exchange hack in late October put the entire crypto market on its toes, prompting crypto platforms to tighten their security.

On November 6, 2026, BNB Chain issued a notice, stating it was aware of the security risk affecting the Reth client. However, its opBNB does not use op-reth, with the impact on BSC-Reth being limited.

On the same day, BNB Chain announced a BSC-Reth hotfix, urging all BSC-Reth node operators on mainnet and testnet to upgrade to v0.1.4-hotfix as soon as possible and restart their nodes.

BNB Chain teases major developments, including its new L1 in development and continued upgrades on BNB Smart Chain (BSC).

Jenner is now on BSC testnet, bringing changes to payments, app timing, and token tools. BEP-703 introduces Payment Lane as part of the Jenner Hardfork on BNB Smart Chain (BSC). It reserves a share of each block's gas capacity for eligible payments, protecting that capacity from general traffic. The default reservation is 5% of each block's gas limit.

Payment Lane sets aside capacity for qualifying BNB transfers between wallets, so unrelated activity cannot use it all. Token payments can also qualify once approved for the lane.

#5236. TeraWulf Says AI Leasing Now Generates 83% of Revenue as Bitcoin Mining Recedes

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: TeraWulf’s November 6 earnings release shows $63 million in third-quarter high-performance-computing lease revenue, around 83% of its $76.2 million total. The company is increasingly an AI infrastructure business, although large non-cash gains and long-dated construction plans require careful reading.

TeraWulf is becoming a clear example of how the economics of Bitcoin mining can lead an infrastructure operator into a rather different business. In its third-quarter results published November 6, the Nasdaq-listed company said high-performance computing, or HPC, generated $63 million of lease revenue in the three months to September 30.

That represented about 83% of its $76.2 million quarterly revenue. Investors who still describe the firm primarily as a Bitcoin miner risk missing where its business is now concentrated and what its future costs may look like.

The company operates existing mining infrastructure at its Lake Mariner campus but is repurposing portions of that site to support contracted computing workloads. TeraWulf said 102 megawatts of revenue-generating critical IT capacity were online at Lake Mariner at quarter-end, with another 336 megawatts under construction.

These numbers are not interchangeable with power agreements or theoretical campus capacity. A contract for future electricity or land does not mean an equivalent amount of paying data-centre capacity is already operating. The distinction matters when a company is projecting revenue from facilities that require years and significant capital to complete.

TeraWulf also reported net income of approximately $709.9 million for the quarter. But it attributed a significant benefit to a non-cash change in the fair value of warrants, while adjusted EBITDA was negative at about $12.8 million. That makes the headline profit a poor substitute for analysing recurring rental revenue, financing costs and operating cash flow.

The company’s release described a 20-year Anthropic lease covering approximately 401 megawatts at its Justified development in Kentucky, representing around $19 billion in contracted revenue over the initial term. It also described a Google credit backstop supporting anticipated project financing and a definitive engineering, procurement and construction agreement with Fluor. These are material commercial commitments, not a claim that $19 billion has already been received.

TeraWulf expects phased service availability at Justified to begin in late 2027 and continue into 2028. At Muskie, it said contracted power capacity had expanded toward one gigawatt, subject to regulatory approval. Schedules, financing and utility infrastructure remain central risks.

NewsBTC has previously examined the mining industry’s move towards AI infrastructure. TeraWulf’s fresh results make that theme measurable: a majority of present revenue now comes from leases rather than mining.

A miner switching to AI can reduce its exposure to Bitcoin production economics, including changes in difficulty, coin price and power cost. It can also create demand for the same grid connections, facilities and financing that historically supported mining. That transition need not be immediately positive or negative for Bitcoin’s network; it depends on which machines are retired, which are redeployed and whether remaining miners take up capacity.

The contrast with NewsBTC’s discussion of rising network hashrate is telling. Competition for mining rewards can intensify even while some large listed companies reshape themselves into computing landlords.

The milestones to watch are completed megawatts, actual rental commencement, capital spending against guidance, contractual counterparty exposure and cash generation. TeraWulf’s results show a dramatic change in revenue mix, but the hardest part of its next chapter will be delivering enormous physical projects reliably and profitably.

#5235. Ripple Joins BlackRock, Standard Chartered in New Tokenization Report

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ripple contributed to a white paper commissioned by the Dubai Financial Services Authority (DFSA), which brings together perspectives and  insights from leading practitioners across the financial services ecosystem.

Ripple's Managing Director, Middle East & Africa, Reece Merrick, recently stated his contribution to  the DFSA's new publication: "Scaling Tokenization: The Road Ahead," alongside leaders from BlackRock, Standard Chartered and Robinhood.

The white paper considers the conditions that may support the responsible scaling of tokenised financial assets and related market infrastructure, including within the Dubai International Financial Centre (DIFC).

Aside from Merrick, other contributors include Tony Ashraf, Managing Director, Head of Technology & Operations and Head of Digital Assets, Middle East & Africa at BlackRock; Coy Garrison, Senior Director and Deputy General Counsel, Crypto at Robinhood; René Michau, Global Head, Digital Asset Advisory at Standard Chartered;  Henson Orser, Founder and Chief  Executive at Soter Insure; and Herman Schueller, Director, Innovation and Technology Risk Supervision at DFSA.

According to the DFSA report, tokenization of traditional financial assets has the potential to become a significant development in mainstream financial markets, offering the prospect of greater access for investors, increased efficiency for issuers and intermediaries, and reduced risk in certain areas, especially around settlement, for the wider financial system.

Merrick shared his perspective, saying that the real shift is not just tokenized treasuries and MMFs (Money Market Funds) gaining AUM but rather their on-chain utility as collateral in repo and margin without needing to redeem to cash first. This viewpoint follows as repo markets continue to move trillions daily, with Merrick highlighting this as potential for tokenization.

Merrick anticipates massive growth in this regard over the next 12 months, predicting that a significant proportion of that will be on the XRP Ledger.

"With clear frameworks, institutional custody, and trusted settlement assets like RLUSD, the UAE, DIFC and Ripple are positioned at the forefront of this next phase," Merrick stated.

Forecasts for the future size of tokenised assets vary considerably. Citi Institute's base case is for the global market in tokenised financial assets to reach $5.5 trillion by 2030.  A Standard Chartered and Synpulse paper estimated that demand for tokenised assets could reach $30.1 trillion by 2034.

#5233. IREN’s Bitcoin Mining Revenue Falls as AI Cloud Sales Nearly Triple

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: IREN reported $200 million in quarterly AI Cloud revenue, up from $71 million the previous quarter, while Bitcoin mining revenue fell from $67 million to $28 million. Its November 5 results underline how quickly some former mining-led businesses are shifting towards AI infrastructure — and how expensive that shift remains.

IREN’s latest quarterly results offer a particularly stark view of the transition taking place across publicly listed Bitcoin infrastructure companies. A November 5 filing with the SEC reports $200 million of AI Cloud revenue for the quarter ended September 30, 2026, compared with $71 million in the preceding quarter.

Bitcoin mining revenue, meanwhile, dropped to $28 million from $67 million. Total revenue rose to $228 million from $137 million, meaning the overall business expanded even as mining became a much smaller share of the total.

The company says AI Cloud revenue increased 182% sequentially and reported an 86% segment gross margin. That is a large change in a single quarter, supported by data-centre capacity coming online and customer contracts. But the term ‘gross margin’ is not a synonym for cash profit after financing, corporate expenses, depreciation and the capital needed to build the infrastructure.

IREN reported a net loss of $263 million for the September quarter, compared with a loss of $684 million in the preceding quarter. Adjusted EBITDA rose to $66 million from $19 million. The coexistence of strong revenue growth and a substantial accounting loss is precisely why neither metric should be read on its own.

The company said its revenue backlog increased to $20.3 billion, about two-thirds of it associated with investment-grade counterparties. A backlog is a contractual forward-looking figure subject to delivery schedules and contract terms; it is not cash already in the bank.

Management said a target of more than $4 billion in annual recurring revenue is fully contracted and funded, but its timetable moved from the end of 2026 to January 2027 following a customer-requested design change and supplier timing. It said completion could extend into February. The distinction between contracted and operating capacity matters especially when large AI computing clusters depend on specialist components and commissioning schedules.

IREN highlighted the Horizon 1 facility entering service for Microsoft and said it had signed additional contracts after quarter-end. Its filing also described planned remaining fiscal-year capital spending and the financing it expects to use. Investors should compare the timing of expenditure with when customer payments are due rather than assuming a huge order book removes funding risks.

The trend resembles the shift described in NewsBTC’s wider examination of Bitcoin miners and AI infrastructure. However, each operator has its own contracting arrangements, retained mining fleet, power infrastructure and financial obligations.

Not necessarily. IREN’s lower mining revenue tells us about one company’s business mix, not the aggregate computing power securing Bitcoin. Network difficulty, global hashrate and other miners’ decisions could move in different directions. NewsBTC’s reporting on hashrate competition is a useful counterpoint to the corporate story.

The next disclosures worth examining are realised AI revenue, actual data-centre commissioning, customer concentration, financing terms and remaining exposure to Bitcoin mining. IREN’s results show the transformation is already reflected in its income statement. Whether the economics produce durable shareholder returns depends on far more than the speed of this quarter’s revenue growth.
